<p class="eh-kicker"><a href="/election">Election Lens</a> · Councillor Stance Profile</p>

# Shawn Lewis

Councillor, Ward 2 (2022–2026 boundaries) · [Full voting record on Open Council →](/councillors/current/s-lewis)

> **This is a descriptive record, not an endorsement.** Every pattern below is built from real recorded votes since 2023, translated from raw yea/nay into what the vote actually did (see [What Council Actually Controls](/election/what-council-controls) for how much of this any of them controls). It says nothing about a councillor's reasons, character, or fitness for office — only how they voted. Votes with no clear direction ("unclear") are excluded from the pattern counts but listed in their own section below for transparency. Every row links to its source: to the heading for the specific agenda item the motion belongs to (several motion parts under one item normally share that one heading — that's how the source pages are laid out, not an error), or, where an item number is reused for two genuinely different, unrelated motions with no way to tell them apart, to the meeting page as a whole instead (the row says so when that happens).

### [Housing & Density](/election/issues/housing)

*Of the 206 divided votes on this issue since 2023 that had a clear direction, this councillor has a recorded position on 202 of them (3 of these are excluded from the pattern counts — see below). 3 absent. The other 1: 1 was a motion with no recorded vote for this councillor at that meeting.*

#### approved the development, rezoning, or housing-policy item vs. denied the development, rezoning, or housing-policy item

Of 95 divided votes across 62 distinct decisions since 2023 where the motion's effect on this axis was clear, this councillor supported 91 measures that would have approved the development, rezoning, or housing-policy item; opposed 1 measure that would have approved the development, rezoning, or housing-policy item; opposed 3 measures that would have denied the development, rezoning, or housing-policy item (absent 2).

#### increased permitted density or building height vs. reduced permitted density or building height

Of 77 divided votes across 49 distinct decisions since 2023 where the motion's effect on this axis was clear, this councillor supported 67 measures that would have increased permitted density or building height; opposed 3 measures that would have increased permitted density or building height; supported 4 measures that would have reduced permitted density or building height; opposed 3 measures that would have reduced permitted density or building height (absent 1).

**3 votes excluded from the pattern above:** this councillor's recorded votes within this decision pointed in different directions across its stages, so none are counted in the tallies; each is listed here:

### [Taxes & Budget](/election/issues/budget)

*Of the 91 divided votes on this issue since 2023 that had a clear direction, this councillor has a recorded position on 86 of them (13 of these are excluded from the pattern counts — see below). 1 absent. The other 4: 1 was a motion at a meeting where this councillor was listed as also present (no vote recorded); 3 were motions with no recorded vote for this councillor at that meeting.*

> Budget votes since 2024 are votes on **amendments** to the Mayor's tabled budget under Ontario's strong-mayor powers, not on an independently council-drafted budget — see [what that changes about what a budget vote means](/election/what-council-controls#who-tables-the-budget).

#### increased the tax levy or a budget item vs. reduced the tax levy or a budget item

Of 63 divided votes across 48 distinct decisions since 2023 where the motion's effect on this axis was clear, this councillor supported 4 measures that would have increased the tax levy or a budget item; opposed 33 measures that would have increased the tax levy or a budget item; supported 15 measures that would have reduced the tax levy or a budget item; opposed 11 measures that would have reduced the tax levy or a budget item (absent 1).

**11 votes excluded from the pattern above:** this councillor's recorded votes within each of these 2 decisions pointed in different directions across their stages, so none are counted in the tallies; each is listed here:

- Yea: Reduced the Parks Maintenance Service Enhancements budget line (Business Case P-31) from $827,000 to $808,000 in 2025 operating expenditures and from $560,000 to $221,000 in 2025 capital expenditures, with a matching reduction to the Climate Change Reserve Fund contribution. (committee stage) — [Amendment - Budget Case No. P-31 - Councillor and Budget Chair E. Peloza and Councillor S. Franke](</months/2024-11/2024-11-22 6th Meeting of the Budget Committee - CONTINUATION#31amendment---budget-case-p-31---councillor-and-budget-chair-e-peloza-and-councillor-s-franke>) (November 22, 2024, counts as against, Motion Passed (13 to 2))
- Yea: Added Parks Maintenance Service Enhancements (5 permanent staff, $613,000 total per Business Case P-31) to the budget, funded via $404,000 in 2025 capital expenditures. ### [Homelessness & Encampments](/election/issues/encampments)

*Of the 65 divided votes on this issue since 2023 that had a clear direction, this councillor has a recorded position on 59 of them (23 of these are excluded from the pattern counts — see below). 1 absent. The other 5: 2 were motions at a meeting where this councillor was listed as also present (no vote recorded); 3 were motions with no recorded vote for this councillor at that meeting.*

#### expanded the encampment/homelessness response vs. reduced the encampment/homelessness response

Of 22 divided votes across 14 distinct decisions since 2023 where the motion's effect on this axis was clear, this councillor supported 19 measures that would have expanded the encampment/homelessness response; supported 2 measures that would have reduced the encampment/homelessness response; opposed 1 measure that would have reduced the encampment/homelessness response.

**22 votes excluded from the pattern above:** this councillor's recorded votes within each of these 6 decisions pointed in different directions across their stages, so none are counted in the tallies; each is listed here:

### [Transit & Roads](/election/issues/transit)

*Of the 60 divided votes on this issue since 2023 that had a clear direction, this councillor has a recorded position on 52 of them (1 of these are excluded from the pattern counts — see below). 1 absent. The other 7: 4 were motions at a meeting where this councillor was listed as also present (no vote recorded); 3 were motions with no recorded vote for this councillor at that meeting.*

#### expanded public transit service or transit infrastructure vs. reduced public transit service or transit infrastructure

Of 43 divided votes across 21 distinct decisions since 2023 where the motion's effect on this axis was clear, this councillor supported 39 measures that would have expanded public transit service or transit infrastructure; opposed 2 measures that would have expanded public transit service or transit infrastructure; supported 1 measure that would have reduced public transit service or transit infrastructure; opposed 1 measure that would have reduced public transit service or transit infrastructure (absent 1).

**1 vote excluded from the pattern above:** on one of these decisions, this councillor's recorded vote was later superseded, the same meeting, by a reconsideration and a re-vote on the identical motion text — the corrected, final vote IS counted in the pattern above (see the evidence table); only the superseded, now-void roll call is excluded, listed here for the record:

- Nay: Would have removed the future capital budgets ($975,000 in 2025 and $4,325,000 in 2026) for the Rapid Transit - East London Link project, funding that is primarily Federal/Provincial PTIS and Development Charges supported. (committee stage) — [Amendment - Rapid Transit - East London Link - Councillor S. ### [Downtown & Core](/election/issues/downtown)

*Of the 19 divided votes on this issue since 2023 that had a clear direction, this councillor has a recorded position on 15 of them (7 of these are excluded from the pattern counts — see below). 1 absent. The other 3: 3 were motions with no recorded vote for this councillor at that meeting.*

#### approved the downtown/core-area item vs. denied the downtown/core-area item

Of 8 divided votes across 5 distinct decisions since 2023 where the motion's effect on this axis was clear, this councillor supported 6 measures that would have approved the downtown/core-area item; supported 2 measures that would have denied the downtown/core-area item (absent 1).

**6 votes excluded from the pattern above:** this councillor's recorded votes within each of these 3 decisions pointed in different directions across their stages, so none are counted in the tallies; each is listed here:

- Yea: Amended the 2026 Annual Budget Update to increase the budget by $784,000 in each of 2026 and 2027 to provide free two-hour on-street parking through the Honk App in the Core Area (Midtown, Downtown, and Old East Village). Ferreira](</months/2025-11/2025-11-20 6th Meeting of the Budget Committee#49added-amendment---councillor-d-ferreira>) (November 20, 2025, counts as for, Motion Passed (11 to 4))
- Nay: Would have amended the 2026 Annual Budget Update to increase the budget by $330,000 in each of 2026 and 2027 to continue a free one-hour on-street parking program through the Honk App in the Core Area (Downtown, Midtown, and Old East Village). (committee stage) — [(ADDED) Amendment - Councillor D. Ferreira](</months/2025-11/2025-11-20 6th Meeting of the Budget Committee#49added-amendment---councillor-d-ferreira>) (November 20, 2025, counts as against, Motion Failed (7 to 8))

### [Climate & Green Space](/election/issues/climate)

*Of the 17 divided votes on this issue since 2023 that had a clear direction, this councillor has a recorded position on 14 of them. The other 3: 1 was a motion at a meeting where this councillor was listed as also present (no vote recorded); 2 were motions with no recorded vote for this councillor at that meeting.*

### [Policing](/election/issues/policing)

*Of the 6 divided votes on this issue since 2023 that had a clear direction, this councillor has a recorded position on 6 of them.*

#### increased the police budget or complement vs. reduced the police budget or complement

Only 4 distinct decisions since 2023 (6 recorded votes across them) — too few to describe a pattern. The individual votes are listed below. No motion on this axis since 2023 would have increased the police budget or complement — every direction-bearing motion here would have reduced the police budget or complement.

---

*Sample sizes above count only votes where Shawn Lewis cast a yea or nay on a motion the verified per-motion classification (see [methodology](/election/issues)) could place on a clear "for"/"against" axis. A recusal means the councillor formally withdrew from discussing and voting on that item — this data does not record why, so no reason is asserted here — and, like an absence, is shown separately in each issue's summary line and never counted as a position. Only sitting councillors get a stance profile on this hub; challengers don't yet have a council voting record to summarize.*

---

Methodology: A "divided" vote is any council or committee motion since January 1, 2023 that wasn't approved unanimously and wasn't a routine procedural step, and it doesn't include a vote decided by secret ballot to fill an appointment, since those don't produce a recorded yea or nay by councillor. For each councillor, on each issue, on each axis: a vote counts as "for" when it matched that axis's more permissive outcome, and "against" when it matched the more restrictive one — but a nay is never described as if it enacted the restrictive outcome; it's described as opposing the motion it was cast on, and the same care is taken in the other direction. When every recorded vote on an axis since January 1, 2023 happens to fall on one side, the pattern sentence says so plainly instead of silently reporting a zero on the other side. Every motion's issue, axis, and direction ("what a yea did") come from an independent, motion-by-motion review, checked against that motion's own complete text in the official meeting record rather than a keyword search, with a small, separately published list of after-the-fact corrections applied on top — never a silent edit to the reviewed record. This is a genuine reading of what each clause did, not a raw yea/nay count, including for the generic "approved or denied the item" description used when no issue-specific axis applies. Recusals and absences are always counted on their own, never folded into "against" and never treated as a hidden position. A pattern sentence is only stated once a councillor has at least 5 separate underlying decisions on an axis — a committee vote followed later by a council vote on the same policy question counts as one decision, not two, when checking that threshold. The "supported/opposed N measures" figure inside the pattern sentence itself counts every recorded vote in the sample, including more than one stage of the same decision, so a motion with several stages CAN make that figure larger than the number of distinct decisions behind it; whenever the two differ, the sentence states the distinct-decision count alongside it. Below the 5-decision threshold, the individual votes are still shown, just without a summary sentence. Some motions were reviewed and confirmed to have no clear direction at all — a referral, a request for more information, or a genuinely ambiguous clause — and these are listed, on every relevant issue, as motions with no clear direction: never counted in any pattern or sample size, but never hidden either. This project has no source recording which committee a councillor belongs to — that list isn't published or collected anywhere; a meeting's own attendance record shows who was there that day, not who belongs to it. So when a councillor has no recorded vote on a motion, nothing is claimed about why, in either direction: only whether that meeting's own record separately listed this councillor as present without a recorded vote, or whether nothing else can be said at all. Before any of the above, motions were also dropped in 2 ways, none of them guessed at or repaired: 31 where the same person was recorded as voting more than one way on the same motion (a roster conflict in the source data); and 14 more that turned out, on review, not to be a genuine division at all — a lopsided result the source data's own "unanimous" marker missed, or the same motion recorded twice under two item numbers. Each motion is filed under exactly one issue, so a motion that touches two topics — a housing decision with a budget line, say — appears on one issue page only, never both. Where the source text has a hash symbol immediately followed by a case or item number — as in a title reading "Business Case", then a hash, then "P-5" — this hub shows it instead as "Business Case No. P-5", so the character can't be misread as a link; the underlying wording is not otherwise changed.
